Model to be displayed.
ToolEditContext_ID_VMDL
Material group (skin) to assign to use with the model.
A weight determining the frequency at which this model is placed relative to other models within the detail type. The weights of all models are summed and the probability of selecting this model is its weight divided by the sum weight.
MPropertyProvidesEditContextString
0.001 1.0
Start fade out size
Screen space size [ 0, 1 ] (where 1 is the whole screen) at which the model will begin to to fade out. Anything larger will be fully visible, anything smaller will start to fade out.
Complete fade out size
Screen space size [ 0, 1 ] (where 1 is the whole screen) at which the model will be completely faded out. Anything smaller than this size will not be visible, anything larger will start fading in.
Use World Space Up
If enabled, the up direction will be evaluated in world space, such that the object orientation does not affect the surface slope filtering. Additionally if Orient To Surface is 0, then and world space up is enabled, then the object orientation will not affect the rotation of detail model.
0.0 1.0
Value indicating if the model's up direction should be matched to the surface. Value should be in the [ 0, 1 ] range, where 0 means that the model up will be model (or object space) up and will ignore the surface direction, and 1 indicates that the model up will exactly match the surface normal.
0.0 180.0
Minimum slope on which the target will be placed. Slope is a [ 0, 180 ] value based on the surface normal where horizontal surface (floor) is 0, vertical surface (wall) is 90, and a horizontal upside down surface (ceiling) is 180.
Maximum slope on which the target will be placed.
Minimum range of random offset to apply along the model's local up direction
Maximum range of random offset to apply along the model's local up direction
Minimum range of the random rotation to apply the model. Random rotation is applied in the local space of the model. Rotation values are ordered pitch, yaw, roll.
Maximum range of the random rotation to apply the model.
Minimum random scale value to apply to the model.
Maximum random scale value to apply to the model.
Density Scale
0.01 1.0
Minimum scale to apply to the model based the painted detail prop density. The minimum of the detail and blend weight scale values is multiplied with the random scale value to determine the final scale.
Blend Weight Scale
Minimum scale to apply to the model based on the final material layer blend weight. The model will be given this scale value when the layer blend weight equals the minimum blend weight and will be 1.0 when the maximum blend weight has been reached. The minimum of the detail and blend weight scale values is multiplied with the random scale value to determine the final scale. Set this if you want the model to scale up as the material blend fades in. The smaller the value, the smaller the model will start as the material begins to fade in.
Min Blend Weight
Minimum blend weight value for which the model will be placed. If the blend weight value is less than this value, the model will not be placed.
Max Blend Weight
Maximum blend weight value for which the model will be placed. If the blend weight value is more than this value, the model will not be placed.
Full Density Blend Weight
Blend weight at which the model will be at full density. Must be between the minimum and maximum blend weight values. The density of this model placement will increase with the blend weight value up to this value at which point it will be at full density.
Should instances of this model generate shadows in the lightmap. Note that shadows in the light map will persist even after the model fades out.

Usage: resourceinfo [-i <resource file name(s)>] [-v] [-q] [-diff_format]
		[-b <block id>]
		[-extract <format> [<output file name>] ]
		[-game <path to gameinfo.txt>]
		[-baremode]
		[-dep] [compile] [runtime]
		[-diff]
		[-module_dep] <module name>
		[-gdep]
		[-maxelements max_elements] 
		[-game_res_kv3] <mod name>
	[ Input ]
		[-f resource-id] 
	              With no additional arguments, a summary of the input(s) will be displayed.
	-i          : Resource file or VPK names; can specify more than one file name.
	[ Displaying Resource Data ]
	-r          : Recursively search directories for files.
	-baremode   : Prints the content of resource block(s) without any info headers.
	-all        : Prints the content of each resource block in the file.
	              Specify the block via its 4CC name - case matters!
	-b          : Print the content of a specific block.
	-raw [file] : Block data is printed as raw bytes (hex dump), optionally to 'file'.
	              (eg. DATA, RERL, REDI, NTRO).
	-crc        : Prints CRC of the file instead of contents.
	-strings    : Prints string dictionary.
	-f          : Finds a resource those resource ID matches the specified one.
	[ Operations ]
	-dep        : If compile is specified, lists all compile-time dependencies.
	-diff       : Compare two files (use -i to specify exactly two files)
	              If neither is specified, both are listed.
	              If runtime is specified, lists all run-time dependencies.
	-gdep       : Lists all resources the game/engine depends on by
	-module_dep : Lists all resources the specified module (DLL) depends on.
	-game_res_kv3 [mod] : Writes a CManifestInfo KV3 file to stdout for all manifests referenced 
	              collating the results of -module_dep for all DLLs. (Respects -v, -q, and -game)
	[ Data Output ]
	                      by the engine DLL's, and game DLL's if mod is specified.  
	                If no format is specified, a list of formats for that file is output.
	-extract [fmt]: Converts a resource into a separate file into the specified format.
	-mip        : Will output all mips of a vtex_c into separate rgba textures
	              (available formats: %s)
	[ Additional Options ]
	-fullmip    : Will output a single rgb image of all mips of a vtex_c showing alpha next to the mip chain
	-q          : Quiet mode
	-v          : Verbose mode
	-game       : Explicitly specify path to a gameinfo.txt file (which mod to use).
	-diff_format: Prepare for diffing, skip absolute paths
